After evaluation completes, it calls `providerRegistry.shutdownAll()` to clean up resources.\n\n**If your provider allocates resources** (Python workers, connections, child processes):\n\n- Implement a `cleanup()` method on your provider\n- Register with `providerRegistry` for automatic cleanup\n- Resources are released in the evaluator's `finally` block\n\n**Reference implementations:**\n\n- `openai.ts` - Most comprehensive\n- `anthropic/index.ts` - Complex provider with subdirectory\n- `http.ts` - Generic HTTP pattern\n\n## Logging\n\nSee `docs/agents/logging.md` - use logger with object context (auto-sanitized).\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n**OpenAI-compatible providers** extend `OpenAiChatCompletionProvider`. See `src/providers/quiverai.ts` for a minimal example or `src/providers/openrouter.ts` for a more complex one.\n\n**Config priority:** Explicit options > Environment variables > Provider defaults\n\n## Provider Routing\n\nPrefix dispatch in `src/providers/registry.ts` is case-sensitive and meaningful. Similar\nprefixes can route to different classes. When you add a new sub-type (e.g.\n`:moderation`, `:embedding`, `:realtime`) to one prefix, do one of:\n\n- Add the sub-type to every prefix it should work under, **or**\n- Explicitly fail-fast (throw with a clear message) for the prefixes that should not\n  support it.\n\nSilently mapping `foo:newtype` to a class that only handles `foo:chat` is a routing\nregression. Add a test in `test/providers/registry.test.ts` that asserts each\nprefix/sub-type pair resolves to the expected class or throws.\n\n## Cache Key Hygiene\n\nPromptfoo's disk cache lives at `${getConfigDirectoryPath()}/cache` (typically\n`~/.promptfoo/cache`), unless overridden with `PROMPTFOO_CACHE_PATH` or\n`PROMPTFOO_CONFIG_DIR`. If an implementation stores literal cache-key strings, those\nstrings persist to disk; if it stores hashed keys, the hash persists.\n\n- **Never include secrets in cache keys.** Strip `Authorization`, bearer tokens, API\n  keys, tenant tokens, signed metadata, and custom auth headers before building or\n  hashing a key. If those values materially change the response, use a non-secret\n  tenant/account identifier, a cache namespace, or disable caching for that path; do not\n  hash raw secrets into long-lived cache keys.\n- **Canonicalize before hashing.** `JSON.stringify({a, b})` and `JSON.stringify({b, a})`\n  produce different strings but represent the same config, so naïve stringification\n  causes cache misses for semantically identical requests. Sort keys (or use a\n  canonical-JSON helper) before hashing.\n- Reference pattern for stable key structure and canonicalized hashing:\n  `getModerationCacheKey` in `src/providers/azure/moderation.ts`.\n\n## Caching Best Practices\n\nWhen implementing caching in your provider, **ALWAYS set the `cached: true` flag** when returning a cached response:\n\n```typescript\n// ✅ CORRECT - Always set cached flag\nif (cachedResponse) {\n  const parsed = JSON.parse(cachedResponse as string);\n  return { ...parsed, cached: true };\n}\n\n// ❌ WRONG - Missing cached flag\nif (cachedResponse) {\n  return JSON.parse(cachedResponse as string);\n}\n```\n\n**Why this matters:**\n\n- The `cached` flag allows downstream code to skip rate limiting delays\n- Performance metrics can distinguish between fresh API calls and cache hits\n- Evaluations can accurately track which responses were served from cache\n\n**Response type compatibility:**\n\n- `ProviderResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n- `ProviderEmbeddingResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n- `ProviderModerationResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n\n**Reference implementations:**\n\n- `bedrock/converse.ts:985` - Sets cached flag correctly\n- `pythonCompletion.ts:194` - Example with spread operator\n- `google/vertex.ts:268` - Multiple cache points handled correctly\n\n## Testing Requirements\n\n**CRITICAL: Tests must NEVER make real API calls.** Mock all HTTP requests.\n\nEvery provider needs tests in `test/providers/`:\n\n- Mock API responses using `vi.mock`\n- Test success AND error cases\n- Test rate limits, timeouts, invalid configs\n- Run with: `npx vitest run test/providers/my-provider`\n\n## Adding a Provider\n\n**All seven items are required** before a provider is complete:\n\n1. Implement `ApiProvider` interface\n2. Add env vars to `src/types/env.ts` (`ProviderEnvOverridesSchema`)\n3. Add env vars to `src/envars.ts` (if documenting in CLI help)\n4. Add tests in `test/providers/`\n5. Add docs in `site/docs/providers/<provider>.md`\n6. Add entry to `site/docs/providers/index.md` table (alphabetical order)\n7. Add example in `examples/<provider>/`\n\nAfter updating env schema, regenerate JSON schema: `npm run jsonSchema:generate`\n\n**Verify completeness:**\n\n```bash\n# Check all pieces exist\nls src/providers/myprovider.ts\ngrep -q \"MYPROVIDER_API_KEY\" src/types/env.ts && echo \"env.ts updated\"\nls test/providers/myprovider.test.ts\nls site/docs/providers/myprovider.md\ngrep -q \"myprovider\" site/docs/providers/index.md && echo \"index.md updated\"\nls examples/myprovider/promptfooconfig.yaml\n```\n\n**Reference existing providers** - 50+ implementations to learn from.","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":145,"totalLines":145,"truncated":false}},"title":"src/providers/AGENTS.md","time":{"start":1782058401803,"end":1782058401872}},"metadata":{"openai":{"itemId":"fc_0c6e5a07297aba89016a380da1a1808190ad2a3f204de9be1f"}},"id":"prt_eeaf54000001ZlFk1eD4B6NJrt","sessionID":"ses_1150b1736ffe2ecHm7xU5m7ISO","messageID":"msg_eeaf5306e001W7GTSp6XjHEXN9"}}

After evaluation completes, it calls `providerRegistry.shutdownAll()` to clean up resources.\n\n**If your provider allocates resources** (Python workers, connections, child processes):\n\n- Implement a `cleanup()` method on your provider\n- Register with `providerRegistry` for automatic cleanup\n- Resources are released in the evaluator's `finally` block\n\n**Reference implementations:**\n\n- `openai.ts` - Most comprehensive\n- `anthropic/index.ts` - Complex provider with subdirectory\n- `http.ts` - Generic HTTP pattern\n\n## Logging\n\nSee `docs/agents/logging.md` - use logger with object context (auto-sanitized).\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n**OpenAI-compatible providers** extend `OpenAiChatCompletionProvider`. See `src/providers/quiverai.ts` for a minimal example or `src/providers/openrouter.ts` for a more complex one.\n\n**Config priority:** Explicit options > Environment variables > Provider defaults\n\n## Provider Routing\n\nPrefix dispatch in `src/providers/registry.ts` is case-sensitive and meaningful. Similar\nprefixes can route to different classes. When you add a new sub-type (e.g.\n`:moderation`, `:embedding`, `:realtime`) to one prefix, do one of:\n\n- Add the sub-type to every prefix it should work under, **or**\n- Explicitly fail-fast (throw with a clear message) for the prefixes that should not\n  support it.\n\nSilently mapping `foo:newtype` to a class that only handles `foo:chat` is a routing\nregression. Add a test in `test/providers/registry.test.ts` that asserts each\nprefix/sub-type pair resolves to the expected class or throws.\n\n## Cache Key Hygiene\n\nPromptfoo's disk cache lives at `${getConfigDirectoryPath()}/cache` (typically\n`~/.promptfoo/cache`), unless overridden with `PROMPTFOO_CACHE_PATH` or\n`PROMPTFOO_CONFIG_DIR`. If an implementation stores literal cache-key strings, those\nstrings persist to disk; if it stores hashed keys, the hash persists.\n\n- **Never include secrets in cache keys.** Strip `Authorization`, bearer tokens, API\n  keys, tenant tokens, signed metadata, and custom auth headers before building or\n  hashing a key. If those values materially change the response, use a non-secret\n  tenant/account identifier, a cache namespace, or disable caching for that path; do not\n  hash raw secrets into long-lived cache keys.\n- **Canonicalize before hashing.** `JSON.stringify({a, b})` and `JSON.stringify({b, a})`\n  produce different strings but represent the same config, so naïve stringification\n  causes cache misses for semantically identical requests. Sort keys (or use a\n  canonical-JSON helper) before hashing.\n- Reference pattern for stable key structure and canonicalized hashing:\n  `getModerationCacheKey` in `src/providers/azure/moderation.ts`.\n\n## Caching Best Practices\n\nWhen implementing caching in your provider, **ALWAYS set the `cached: true` flag** when returning a cached response:\n\n```typescript\n// ✅ CORRECT - Always set cached flag\nif (cachedResponse) {\n  const parsed = JSON.parse(cachedResponse as string);\n  return { ...parsed, cached: true };\n}\n\n// ❌ WRONG - Missing cached flag\nif (cachedResponse) {\n  return JSON.parse(cachedResponse as string);\n}\n```\n\n**Why this matters:**\n\n- The `cached` flag allows downstream code to skip rate limiting delays\n- Performance metrics can distinguish between fresh API calls and cache hits\n- Evaluations can accurately track which responses were served from cache\n\n**Response type compatibility:**\n\n- `ProviderResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n- `ProviderEmbeddingResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n- `ProviderModerationResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n\n**Reference implementations:**\n\n- `bedrock/converse.ts:985` - Sets cached flag correctly\n- `pythonCompletion.ts:194` - Example with spread operator\n- `google/vertex.ts:268` - Multiple cache points handled correctly\n\n## Testing Requirements\n\n**CRITICAL: Tests must NEVER make real API calls.** Mock all HTTP requests.\n\nEvery provider needs tests in `test/providers/`:\n\n- Mock API responses using `vi.mock`\n- Test success AND error cases\n- Test rate limits, timeouts, invalid configs\n- Run with: `npx vitest run test/providers/my-provider`\n\n## Adding a Provider\n\n**All seven items are required** before a provider is complete:\n\n1. Implement `ApiProvider` interface\n2. Add env vars to `src/types/env.ts` (`ProviderEnvOverridesSchema`)\n3. Add env vars to `src/envars.ts` (if documenting in CLI help)\n4. Add tests in `test/providers/`\n5. Add docs in `site/docs/providers/<provider>.md`\n6. Add entry to `site/docs/providers/index.md` table (alphabetical order)\n7. Add example in `examples/<provider>/`\n\nAfter updating env schema, regenerate JSON schema: `npm run jsonSchema:generate`\n\n**Verify completeness:**\n\n```bash\n# Check all pieces exist\nls src/providers/myprovider.ts\ngrep -q \"MYPROVIDER_API_KEY\" src/types/env.ts && echo \"env.ts updated\"\nls test/providers/myprovider.test.ts\nls site/docs/providers/myprovider.md\ngrep -q \"myprovider\" site/docs/providers/index.md && echo \"index.md updated\"\nls examples/myprovider/promptfooconfig.yaml\n```\n\n**Reference existing providers** - 50+ implementations to learn unknown;\n  is_error: boolean;\n  parentToolUseId: string | null;\n}\n\n/** Hard cap for attribute body length on synthesized tool spans. */\nconst TOOL_SPAN_BODY_LIMIT = 4096;\n\n/**\n * Append promptfoo-specific resource-attribute kvs to a W3C-style\n * `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` string, removing trailing whitespace/commas from\n * the existing value and stripping any previous occurrence of our keys so the\n * producer can't double-up. Returns the new string. Exported for tests.\n */\nexport function appendPromptfooResourceAttrs(\n  existing: string | undefined,\n  traceId: string,\n  parentSpanId: string,\n): string {\n  const incoming = `${PROMPTFOO_RESOURCE_ATTR_TRACE_ID}=${traceId},${PROMPTFOO_RESOURCE_ATTR_PARENT_SPAN_ID}=${parentSpanId}`;\n  if (!existing) {\n    return incoming;\n  }\n  const cleaned = existing\n    .split(',')\n    .map((pair) => pair.trim())\n    .filter(\n      (pair) =>\n        pair.length > 0 &&\n        !pair.startsWith(`${PROMPTFOO_RESOURCE_ATTR_TRACE_ID}=`) &&\n        !pair.startsWith(`${PROMPTFOO_RESOURCE_ATTR_PARENT_SPAN_ID}=`),\n    )\n    .join(',');\n  return cleaned.length > 0 ? `${cleaned},${incoming}` : incoming;\n}\n\nfunction stringifyForSpan(value: unknown): string | undefined {\n  if (value === undefined || value === null) {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n  let raw: string | undefined;\n  if (typeof value === 'string') {\n    raw = value;\n  } else {\n    try {\n      raw = JSON.stringify(value);\n    } catch {\n      // JSON.stringify throws on circular references / BigInts / etc.\n      // Preserve the enclosing span by substituting a sentinel for just this attribute.\n      return '<unserializable>';\n    }\n  }\n  if (raw === undefined) {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n  const sanitized = sanitizeBody(raw);\n  return sanitized.length > TOOL_SPAN_BODY_LIMIT\n    ? `${sanitized.slice(0, TOOL_SPAN_BODY_LIMIT - 15)}... [truncated]`\n    : sanitized;\n}\n\n/**\n * Emit a child span for a single tool call. Parents to the currently active\n * span (the provider's GenAI wrapper) so the UI shows an agent → tool hierarchy\n * similar to the OpenAI Agents SDK.\n *\n * When `incomplete` is true the call never produced a matching `tool_result`\n * (aborted run, stop hook, or the stream ended mid-tool). Such spans are\n * flagged via `tool.incomplete` and marked ERROR so traces surface the gap\n * instead of silently dropping the tool.\n */\nfunction emitToolSpan(\n  entry: ToolCallEntry,\n  startTimeMs: number,\n  endTimeMs: number,\n  isError: boolean,\n  incomplete = false,\n): void {\n  try {\n    const tracer = getGenAITracer();\n    const attributes: Record<string, string | number | boolean> = {\n      'tool.name': entry.name,\n      'tool.is_error': isError,\n    };\n    if (incomplete) {\n      attributes['tool.incomplete'] = true;\n    }\n    const input = stringifyForSpan(entry.input);\n    if (input !== undefined) {\n      attributes['tool.input'] = input;\n    }\n    const output = stringifyForSpan(entry.output);\n    if (output !== undefined) {\n      attributes['tool.output'] = output;\n    }\n    if (entry.parentToolUseId) {\n      attributes['tool.parent_id'] = entry.parentToolUseId;\n    }\n\n    const span = tracer.startSpan(`tool ${entry.name}`, {\n      startTime: startTimeMs,\n      attributes,\n    });\n    span.setStatus({\n      code: isError || incomplete ? SpanStatusCode.ERROR : SpanStatusCode.OK,\n    });\n    span.end(endTimeMs);\n  } catch (err) {\n    logger.warn(`[ClaudeAgentSDK] Failed to emit tool span for ${entry.name}: ${err}`);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction deriveSkillCalls(toolCalls: ToolCallEntry[]): SkillCallEntry[] {\n  return toolCalls\n    .filter((toolCall) => toolCall.name === 'Skill')\n    .flatMap((toolCall) => {\n      const skillName =\n        toolCall.input &&\n        typeof toolCall.input === 'object' &&\n        typeof (toolCall.input as Record<string, unknown>).skill === 'string'\n          ? ((toolCall.input as Record<string, unknown>).skill as string).trim()\n          : '';\n\n      if (!skillName) {\n        return [];\n      }\n\n      return [\n        {\n          name: skillName,\n          input: toolCall.input,\n          is_error: toolCall.is_error,\n          source: 'tool' as const,\n        },\n      ];\n    });\n}\n\n/**\n * Claude Agent SDK Provider\n *\n * This provider requires the @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk package to be installed separately:\n *   npm install @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk\n *\n * Two default configurations:\n * - No working_dir: Runs in temp directory with no tools - behaves like plain chat API\n * - With working_dir: Runs in specified directory with read-only file tools (Read/Grep/Glob/LS)\n *\n * User can override tool permissions with 'custom_allowed_tools', 'append_allowed_tools', 'disallowed_tools', and 'permission_mode'.\n *\n * For side effects (file writes, system calls, etc.), user can override permissions and use a custom working directory. They're then responsible for setup/teardown and security considerations.\n *\n * MCP server connection details are passed through from config. strict_mcp_config is true by default to only allow explicitly configured MCP servers.\n */\n\n// When a working directory is provided, we allow read-only tools by default (when no working directory is provided, default to no tools)\nexport const FS_READONLY_ALLOWED_TOOLS = ['Read', 'Grep', 'Glob', 'LS'].sort(); // sort and export for tests\n\n// Claude Agent SDK supports these model aliases in addition to full model names\n// See: https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/claude-agent-sdk/model-config\nexport const CLAUDE_CODE_MODEL_ALIASES = [\n  'default',\n  'sonnet',\n  'opus',\n  'haiku',\n  'sonnet[1m]',\n  'opusplan',\n];\n\n/**\n * Helper to load the Claude Agent SDK ESM module\n * Uses resolvePackageEntryPoint to handle ESM-only packages with restrictive exports\n */\nasync function loadClaudeCodeSDK(): Promise<typeof import('@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk')> {\n  const basePath =\n    cliState.basePath && path.isAbsolute(cliState.basePath) ? cliState.basePath : process.cwd();\n\n  const claudeCodePath = resolvePackageEntryPoint('@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk', basePath);\n\n  if (!claudeCodePath) {\n    throw new Error(\n      dedent`The @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk package could not be resolved from ${basePath}.\n\n      To use the Claude Agent SDK provider, install it with:\n        npm install @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk\n\n      If the package is already installed elsewhere, run promptfoo from the\n      project root (or point the config at that root) so node_modules is on\n      the resolution path.\n\n      For more information, see: https://www.promptfoo.dev/docs/providers/claude-agent-sdk/`,\n    );\n  }\n\n  try {\n    return importModule(claudeCodePath);\n  } catch (err) {\n    logger.error(`Failed to load Claude Agent SDK: ${err}`);","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":260,"totalLines":1660,"truncated":true}},"title":"src/providers/claude-agent-sdk.ts","time":{"start":1782058406708,"end":1782058406804}},"metadata":{"openai":{"itemId":"fc_0d851a3d685db0d9016a380da68c4c81978f176fc400eb2fd9"}},"id":"prt_eeaf55329001bhUPcr6J7nieBQ","sessionID":"ses_1150b1736ffe2ecHm7xU5m7ISO","messageID":"msg_eeaf544a8001JHPhiG5NgH5euq"}}

Precedence is documented on the\n    // `env` field of ClaudeCodeOptions: process.env < config.env < EnvOverrides.\n    const env: Record<string, string> = {};\n    for (const key of Object.keys(process.env).sort()) {\n      if (process.env[key] !== undefined) {\n        env[key] = process.env[key];\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (config.env) {\n      for (const key of Object.keys(config.env).sort()) {\n        const value = config.env[key];\n        if (value !== undefined) {\n          env[key] = value;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (this.env) {\n      for (const key of Object.keys(this.env).sort()) {\n        const value = this.env[key as keyof typeof this.env];\n        if (value !== undefined) {\n          env[key] = value;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Ensure API key is available to Claude Agent SDK\n    if (this.apiKey) {\n      env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY = this.apiKey;\n    }\n\n    // Could potentially do more to validate credentials for Bedrock/Vertex here, but Anthropic key is the main use case\n    if (\n      !this.apiKey &&\n      !(\n        config.apiKeyRequired === false ||\n        env.CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK ||\n        env.CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX\n      )\n    ) {\n      throw new Error(\n        dedent`Anthropic API key is not set. Prefer the active span's traceparent; fall back to the\n          // evaluator-supplied one. `getTraceparent()` can return an all-zero trace\n          // id when no OTEL SDK is registered (NonRecordingSpan / INVALID_TRACEID),\n          // which would poison propagation — skip it in that case.\n          const ZERO_TRACE_ID = '00000000000000000000000000000000';\n          const active = getTraceparent();\n          const activeValid = active && !active.includes(ZERO_TRACE_ID) ? active : undefined;\n          const traceparent = activeValid ?? context?.traceparent;\n          // Mutating env here is safe because initializeAgenticCache serialized\n          // cacheKeyQueryOptions (which shares this env reference) into a hash\n          // string earlier in callApi, so TRACEPARENT cannot affect the cache key.\n          if (traceparent && !env.TRACEPARENT) {\n            env.TRACEPARENT = traceparent;\n          }\n          // Some SDK telemetry signals (logs in particular) do not inherit\n          // TRACEPARENT into their OTEL context. Encode the trace + parent span\n          // IDs as resource attributes too — promptfoo's OTLP /v1/logs receiver\n          // reads these to link log-derived spans to the evaluation trace.\n          // traceparent format: \"version-traceId-spanId-flags\".\n          const [, tpTraceId, tpSpanId] = traceparent ? traceparent.split('-') : [];\n          if (tpTraceId && tpSpanId) {\n            env.OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES = appendPromptfooResourceAttrs(\n              env.OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES,\n              tpTraceId,\n              tpSpanId,\n            );\n          }\n\n          // Dynamically import the ESM module once and cache it\n          if (!this.claudeCodeModule) {\n            this.claudeCodeModule = await loadClaudeCodeSDK();\n          }\n\n          const res = await this.claudeCodeModule.query(queryParams);\n\n          // Collect tool calls and results from intermediate messages\n          const toolCallsMap = new Map<string, ToolCallEntry>();\n          // Wall-clock start time per tool_use.id, captured when we first see the tool_use\n          // message so we can synthesize child spans with realistic durations.\n          const toolStartTimes = new Map<string, number>();\n\n          // Drain any tool_use entries that never saw a matching tool_result. // Prevents test pollution\n});\n```\n\n## Directory Structure\n\nTests mirror `src/` structure:\n\n- `test/providers/` → `src/providers/`\n- `test/redteam/` → `src/redteam/`\n- `test/agentSkills/` → agent plugin contract tests; read `test/agentSkills/AGENTS.md`\n- `test/fixtures/agent-skills/` → runnable agent skill fixtures; read `test/fixtures/agent-skills/AGENTS.md`\n\n## Mocking\n\n```typescript\nimport { vi } from 'vitest';\n\nvi.mock('axios');\nconst axiosMock = vi.mocked(axios);\naxiosMock.post.mockResolvedValue({ data: { result: 'success' } });\n```\n\n- Use Vitest's mocking utilities (`vi.mock`, `vi.fn`, `vi.spyOn`)\n- Prefer shallow mocking over deep mocking\n- Mock external dependencies but not the code being tested\n- Reset mocks between tests to prevent test pollution\n\n**Critical: Mock Isolation**\n\n`vi.clearAllMocks()` only clears call history, NOT mock implementations. Use `mockReset()` for full isolation:\n\n```typescript\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks(); // Clears .mock.calls and .mock.results\n  vi.mocked(myMock).mockReset(); // Also clears mockReturnValue/mockResolvedValue\n});\n```\n\nFor `vi.hoisted()` mocks or mocks with `mockReturnValue()`, you MUST call `mockReset()` in `beforeEach` to ensure test isolation when tests run in random order.\n\n## Environment Variables\n\nPrefer `mockProcessEnv()` from `test/util/utils.ts` for root tests that need to change environment variables. Use `vi.stubEnv()` only when a test specifically needs Vitest's stub behavior, and pair it with `vi.unstubAllEnvs()`.\n\nAvoid direct `process.env.FOO = ...`, `delete process.env.FOO`, or `process.env = ...` mutations in new tests. The root hygiene suite blocks new files that use direct environment mutation because tests run in random order.\n\n## Zustand Store Testing\n\nWhen testing components that use Zustand stores, prefer **integration testing with real stores** over mocking.\n\n### When to Use Real Stores vs Mocking\n\n**Use real stores (preferred)** when:\n\n- Testing components that modify store state\n- Verifying state changes after user interactions\n- Testing store action logic (merge, update, delete)\n\n**Mock the store** only when:\n\n- Testing pure UI components that only read from stores\n- Testing isolated component logic completely unrelated to state\n\n### Pattern: Integration Testing with Real Stores\n\n**Reference**: `src/app/src/pages/redteam/setup/components/PluginsTab.test.tsx`\n\n```typescript\nimport { act, render, screen, waitFor } from '@testing-library/react';\nimport userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';\nimport { beforeEach, afterEach, describe, expect, test, vi } from 'vitest';\nimport { useMyStore } from './useMyStore';\n\n// Capture initial state OUTSIDE describe block\nconst initialState = useMyStore.getState();\n\ndescribe('MyComponent', () => {\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    // Reset store to initial state before each test\n    act(() => {\n      useMyStore.setState(initialState);\n    });\n  });\n\n  afterEach(() => {\n    // Clean up after each test\n    act(() => {\n      useMyStore.setState(initialState);\n    });\n  });\n\n  test('user interaction updates store correctly', async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n\n    // Verify initial state\n    expect(useMyStore.getState().items).toHaveLength(0);\n\n    render(<MyComponent />);\n\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add Item' }));\n\n    // Assert on actual store state, not mock calls\n    await waitFor(() => {\n      expect(useMyStore.getState().items).toHaveLength(1);\n    });\n  });\n});\n```\n\n### Key Patterns\n\n1. **Capture initial state outside describe**: `const initialState = useMyStore.getState();`\n2. **Reset in beforeEach AND afterEach**: Ensures test isolation\n3. **Wrap setState in act()**: Required for React state batching\n4. **Assert on getState()**: Verify actual store state, not that a mock was called\n5. **Use waitFor for async**: Store updates may be asynchronous\n6. **Mock external dependencies only**: Mock APIs, analytics, child components—NOT the store\n\n### Anti-Pattern: Mocking the Store (avoid this)\n\n```typescript\n// ❌ AVOID: Mocking the store hook loses integration coverage\nvi.mock('./useMyStore');\nconst mockUpdateItems = vi.fn();\n(useMyStore as any).mockReturnValue({\n  items: [],\n  updateItems: mockUpdateItems,\n});\n\ntest('clicking button calls updateItems', () => {\n  render(<MyComponent />);\n  fireEvent.click(button);\n  // Only verifies mock was called, not that store logic works\n  expect(mockUpdateItems).toHaveBeenCalled();\n});\n```\n\n### Additional Store Test References\n\n- `src/app/src/store/providersStore.test.ts` - Basic store testing\n- `src/app/src/stores/evalConfig.test.ts` - Configuration state\n- `src/app/src/stores/userStore.test.ts` - Async operations with act()\n\n## Provider Testing\n\nEvery provider needs tests covering:\n\n1. Success case\n2. Error cases (4xx, 5xx, rate limits)\n3. Configuration validation\n4. Token usage tracking\n\nWhen a change introduces request-format gating, parser selection, or middleware validation, add negative-path tests for malformed inputs and assert the exact response contract.\n\nSee `test/providers/openai-codex-sdk.test.ts` for reference patterns.\n\n## Smoke Tests\n\nSmoke tests verify the **built CLI package** works correctly end-to-end. They test `dist/src/main.js` directly using `spawnSync`.\n\n```bash\n# Run smoke tests\nnpm run test:smoke\n\n# Run specific smoke test file\nnpx vitest run test/smoke/cli.test.ts --config vitest.smoke.config.ts\n```\n\n**Location:** `test/smoke/` with fixtures in `test/smoke/fixtures/configs/`\n\n**Reference pattern** (from `test/smoke/filters-and-flags.test.ts`):\n\n```typescript\nimport { spawnSync } from 'child_process';\nimport * as fs from 'fs';\nimport * as path from 'path';\nimport { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';\n\nconst CLI_PATH = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../dist/src/main.js');\nconst FIXTURES_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, 'fixtures/configs');\nconst OUTPUT_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, '.temp-output');\n\nfunction runCli(args: string[], options: { cwd?: string } = {}) {\n  const result = spawnSync('node', [CLI_PATH, ...args], {\n    cwd: options.cwd || path.resolve(__dirname, '../..'),\n    encoding: 'utf-8',\n    env: { ...process.env, NO_COLOR: '1' },\n    timeout: 60000,\n  });\n  return {\n    stdout: result.stdout || '',\n    stderr: result.stderr || '',\n    exitCode: result.status ?? 1,\n  };\n}\n\ndescribe('My Smoke Tests', () => {\n  beforeAll(() => {\n    if (!fs.existsSync(CLI_PATH)) {\n      throw new Error(`Built CLI not found. Run 'npm run build' first.`);\n    }\n    fs.mkdirSync(OUTPUT_DIR, { recursive: true });\n  });\n\n  afterAll(() => {\n    fs.rmSync(OUTPUT_DIR, { recursive: true, force: true });\n  });\n\n  it('runs eval with echo provider', () => {\n    const configPath = path.join(FIXTURES_DIR, 'basic.yaml');\n    const outputPath = path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, 'output.json');\n\n    const { exitCode } = runCli(['eval', '-c', configPath, '-o', outputPath, '--no-cache']);\n\n    expect(exitCode).toBe(0);\n    const parsed = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(outputPath, 'utf-8'));\n    expect(parsed.results.results[0].response.output).toContain('Hello');\n  });\n});\n```\n\n**Key patterns:**\n\n- Test the **built package** (`dist/src/main.js`), not source code\n- Use `spawnSync` with `node` to run the CLI directly\n- Fixtures go in `test/smoke/fixtures/configs/` (committed to git)\n- Temp output directories cleaned up in `afterAll`\n- Use `echo` provider for deterministic, zero-cost testing\n- Check exit codes AND output file contents\n\n**Fixture example** (`test/smoke/fixtures/configs/basic.yaml`):\n\n```yaml\nproviders:\n  - echo\n\nprompts:\n  - 'Hello {{name}}'\n\ntests:\n  - vars:\n      name: World\n    assert:\n      - type: contains\n        value: World\n```\n\nSee `docs/plans/smoke-tests.md` for the full test checklist.\n\n## Test Configuration\n\n- Config: `vitest.config.ts` (unit tests), `vitest.integration.config.ts` (integration tests), `vitest.smoke.config.ts` (smoke tests)\n- Setup: `vitest.setup.ts`\n- Globals disabled: All test utilities must be explicitly imported from `vitest`\n- Import `describe`, `it`, `expect`, `beforeEach`, `afterEach`, `vi` from `vitest`\n\n## Testing Code with Timers\n\nWhen testing code that uses `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, or `Date.now()`, use **fake timers** to make tests deterministic and fast.\n\n**Reference files:**\n\n- `test/scheduler/slotQueue.test.ts` - Comprehensive fake timer usage\n- `test/scheduler/providerRateLimitState.test.ts` - Async code with timers\n\n### Basic Pattern\n\n```typescript\nimport { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';\n\ndescribe('MyTimerCode', () => {\n  beforeEach(() => {\n    vi.useFakeTimers();\n  });\n\n  afterEach(() => {\n    vi.useRealTimers();\n  });\n\n  it('handles timeout', () => {\n    const callback = vi.fn();\n    setTimeout(callback, 1000);\n\n    vi.advanceTimersByTime(1000);\n\n    expect(callback).toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n});\n```\n\n### Async Code with Timers\n\nWhen testing async functions that internally use timers, you cannot simply `await` them—the promise will hang because fake timers don't advance automatically. Use this pattern:\n\n```typescript\nit('handles async retry with delay', async () => {\n  // 1. Start the promise (don't await yet)\n  const promise = myAsyncFunctionWithRetry();\n\n  // 2. Run all timers to completion\n  await vi.runAllTimersAsync();\n\n  // 3. Now await the result\n  const result = await promise;\n\n  expect(result).toBe('success');\n});\n```\n\n### Why Fake Timers Matter\n\nReal timers cause flaky tests because:\n\n- **Timer resolution varies**: Windows has ~15ms minimum resolution vs ~1-4ms on Linux/Mac\n- **Race conditions**: Code checking `Date.now()` multiple times can see different values if a millisecond boundary is crossed\n- **CI variability**: Tests may pass locally but fail under CI load\n\n**Anti-pattern** (causes flaky tests):\n\n```typescript\n// ❌ AVOID: Real timers with small delays\nit('retries after delay', async () => {\n  const result = await functionThatRetries({ retryAfterMs: 1 }); // May timeout randomly\n});\n```\n\n**Correct pattern**:\n\n```typescript\n// ✓ CORRECT: Fake timers with controlled advancement\nit('retries after delay', async () => {\n  vi.useFakeTimers();\n  const promise = functionThatRetries({ retryAfterMs: 1000 });\n  await vi.runAllTimersAsync();\n  const result = await promise;\n  vi.useRealTimers();\n});\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n- Ensure all tests are independent and can run in any order\n- Clean up any test data or mocks after each test\n- Run the full test suite before committing changes\n- Test failures should be deterministic\n- For database tests, use in-memory instances or proper test fixtures\n- **Use fake timers** for any code involving `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, or timing-sensitive logic\n\n</system-reminder>","metadata":{"preview":"        // When structured output is available, it should be the output\n
